Can we show them those archived records data directly on case opp record page without lwc ? Like via widget or any other way ?

6:45 AM
Claude responded: Good.

Searched the web
Searched the web
Good. Now I have a clear picture. Let me give you the complete honest answer.

THE CORE QUESTION

Can you show archived content on the Case/Opportunity record page WITHOUT an LWC component?

Every option explored, with exactly what works and what does not.

OPTION 1: VISUALFORCE PAGE DEPLOYED VIA METADATA API

This is the closest thing to "without LWC" that actually works inline on the record page.

Visualforce pages (ApexPage) are a supported Metadata API component type — you can deploy them via the standard deploy() call. 
Salesforce

What this means: during your org setup (the same step where you deploy custom fields), you also deploy a Visualforce page + Apex controller via Metadata API. The client org gets them without installing any package.

The Visualforce page makes a callout to your Hono API, gets the archived email HTML, and renders it in an iframe inside the page.

But here is the critical constraint: A Visualforce page cannot be added as its own Lightning component on a Lightning record page directly. However, if the Lightning page layout uses the Classic page layout as a component, the Visualforce page will render as part of the Record Detail component. 
Mode

So the exact behaviour depends on how the client's Case page is set up:

Lightning Record Page with "Record Detail" component:
  → You can embed a VF page in the Classic page layout
  → It renders inside the Record Detail section
  → Looks slightly dated but works inline

Lightning Record Page WITHOUT Classic Record Detail:
  → VF page cannot be placed directly as a standalone component
  → Only option is adding it as a related list or separate tab
Most Service Cloud orgs running Lightning Experience use the classic Record Detail component inside their Lightning page. This is still the majority. So this approach works for most clients.

Does it require Apex? Yes, and here is why that matters.

Salesforce does not allow creating Apex classes via Metadata API from a non-certified managed package. Apex Metadata API deployments require either a certified managed package or a specific Apex setting enabled by the subscriber org. 
Salesforce Developers

Wait — that quote is about Apex Metadata API (Apex code calling the Metadata API). That is different from the standard Metadata API deploy. Let me be precise:

The standard Metadata API (what your Hono backend calls via HTTP) CAN deploy Apex classes and Visualforce pages to any org. This is what every CI/CD tool (Gearset, Copado, etc.) does every day. Deploying Lightning pages via Metadata API is the same as any other metadata deployment — select the components, deploy, done. 
Marktyrrell

The restriction only applies to Apex code calling the Metadata API (Apex Metadata API — an in-org feature). Your backend uses the external Metadata API over HTTP, which has no such restriction.

However — deploying Apex classes requires test coverage. SF requires 75% test coverage for Apex in production. When you deploy an Apex controller via Metadata API to a production org, SF runs your test classes. You must include a test class with 75%+ coverage in your deployment package.

This is solvable but adds complexity. A simple Apex controller for a VF page that just makes an HTTP callout is maybe 30 lines. A test class for it is another 20 lines. Totally doable.

OPTION 2: VISUALFORCE PAGE WITHOUT APEX — PURE JAVASCRIPT CALLOUT

You do not actually need Apex for this. A Visualforce page can make callouts directly from JavaScript using the session token from the page context. No Apex controller needed at all.

html
<apex:page standardController="Case" showHeader="false" sidebar="false">
<script>
  // Get Case ID and Session ID from VF merge fields
  var caseId = '{!Case.Id}';
  var sessionId = '{!$Api.Session_ID}';
  var isArchived = '{!Case.vault__Is_Archived__c}';
  
  if (isArchived === 'true') {
    // Call your Hono API directly from JavaScript
    fetch('https://vault.com/api/view/' + caseId, {
      headers: { 'Authorization': 'Bearer ' + sessionId }
    })
    .then(r => r.text())
    .then(html => {
      document.getElementById('vault-container').innerHTML = html;
    });
  }
</script>
<div id="vault-container">
  <apex:outputPanel rendered="{!Case.vault__Is_Archived__c}">
    Loading archived content...
  </apex:outputPanel>
  <apex:outputPanel rendered="{!!Case.vault__Is_Archived__c}">
    <!-- Empty — Case not archived -->
  </apex:outputPanel>
</div>
</apex:page>
This is pure Visualforce with no Apex controller. Deployable via Metadata API with zero Apex. No test classes needed. No test coverage requirement.

The standardController="Case" gives you merge field access ({!Case.Id}, {!Case.vault__Is_Archived__c}, {!$Api.Session_ID}) without any custom Apex.

However — X-Frame-Options is your enemy here. Salesforce sets X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN on its pages, which means your vault.com responses need to NOT set that header, and Salesforce renders the VF page inline rather than in an iframe. Actually the fetch + innerHTML approach above avoids the iframe problem entirely — you are fetching HTML and injecting it into the DOM, not using an iframe.

OPTION 3: LIGHTNING FLEXIPAGE COMPONENT — THE SNEAKY ONE

Lightning Pages (FlexiPage in Metadata API) are deployable via Metadata API and can be assigned as org-default or app-specific page layouts. 
UnofficialSF

Here is what this means for you. You can deploy a complete Lightning Record Page configuration that includes a Visualforce page component embedded in the page layout. This FlexiPage can be activated as the org-default Case page — replacing whatever the admin had before — or it can just be deployed as an available page they can activate manually.

This is the path some ISVs take: deploy a pre-configured FlexiPage with your VF component already placed in it. The admin sees it as an option in Lightning App Builder and activates it with one click.

Downside: if the client has a heavily customised Case page, you cannot overwrite it automatically. You would need to add your VF component to their existing FlexiPage, which requires reading their current FlexiPage XML, modifying it, and redeploying — more fragile.

OPTION 4: SALESFORCE CANVAS APP

Canvas apps allow you to embed Node.js/web applications directly inside Salesforce pages via Visualforce. The Canvas app can be placed on the Case page layout and shows a web page within a secure signed request context. 
Salesforce

This is what GRAX's "Embedded Experience" actually uses. You define a Canvas App in your Connected App, and it renders your external web app (vault.com) in a frame inside Salesforce. The frame receives a signed request containing the SF user's session data, record ID, and org info.

The problem: Canvas requires adding a Visualforce page that hosts the Canvas component, or the admin must manually place the Canvas app in the page layout via drag-and-drop in App Builder. There is no way to auto-place a Canvas app without either a managed package or admin manual action.

Also, Canvas is being de-emphasised by Salesforce. It still works but is not their recommended direction.

HONEST SUMMARY OF ALL OPTIONS

THE ACTUAL RECOMMENDED APPROACH

Phase 1 (no manual SF steps, ships now): Custom button. Opens vault.com in new tab. Already designed, already in your Metadata API deployment. Works for everyone. Zero complexity.

Phase 1.5 (one manual step, deployable via Metadata API): Deploy a Visualforce page (no Apex controller) during org setup alongside the custom fields. Then your onboarding checklist gets one more guided step:

□ Add Vault viewer to your Case layout
  
  1. Go to Setup → Object Manager → Case → Page Layouts
  2. Find "Vault Archived Content" in the Visualforce Pages section
  3. Drag it onto your layout under the Email related list area
  4. Set height to 400px
  5. Save
  
  [I've added the component →]
This gives full inline viewing on the Case page, no package, no Apex, no LWC — just a VF page you deployed and the admin drags into place in 60 seconds.

Phase 2 (fully automatic, no manual steps): LWC managed package. Deploy via install URL. Auto-places itself on the page layout during installation. Zero admin manual steps after clicking install.

The VF page approach is genuinely good enough for most clients and most will not notice or care that it is Visualforce rather than LWC. The email thread renders as HTML inside the VF section. It looks native. GRAX built their entire embedded experience on this same pattern.

Understood. You want zero context switch — agent opens the Case, sees everything right there. No button, no new tab, no "go somewhere else."

Let me think through every option that achieves this without LWC.

THE ONLY WAYS TO SHOW CONTENT INLINE ON A RECORD PAGE

1. LWC component on page layout          → requires managed package
2. Visualforce page on page layout       → deployable via Metadata API
3. Aura component on page layout         → same as LWC, requires package
4. Canvas App on page layout             → semi-deprecated, still works
5. Flow Screen on page layout            → limited, not suitable for rich HTML
6. Override the record page entirely     → too destructive, not viable
Everything that shows inline on a Case page without a managed package is either Visualforce or Canvas. LWC is the only truly "no compromises" option but needs a package.

The Visualforce path is the right answer for Phase 1. Let me show you exactly how it works inline so you understand what the agent actually sees.

WHAT THE AGENT SEES — VISUALFORCE INLINE ON CASE PAGE

The agent never leaves Salesforce. Never clicks a button to go elsewhere. It is just there, on the page, like a related list — but showing email content.

HOW IT WORKS TECHNICALLY

The Visualforce page is a section on the Case page layout. It loads automatically when the agent opens the Case. It calls your Hono API, gets the archived data, renders it. The agent sees it within 1-2 seconds of opening the page.

Agent opens Case #00012345
        ↓
Lightning page loads — Case fields, related lists, etc.
        ↓
Visualforce section loads (like any other VF section on a layout)
        ↓
VF page JavaScript runs:
  1. Reads {!Case.Id} and {!Case.vault__Is_Archived__c} from page context
  2. If Is_Archived__c = false → shows nothing (invisible, 0 height)
  3. If Is_Archived__c = true → calls your API
        ↓
  fetch('https://vault.com/api/sf-inline/{caseId}', {
    headers: {
      'Authorization': 'Bearer {!$Api.Session_ID}',
      'X-Org-Id': '{!$Organization.Id}'
    }
  })
        ↓
Your Hono API:
  1. Validates SF session token via SF identity endpoint
  2. Matches org ID to tenant in D1
  3. Reads email JSONs from R2
  4. Reads attachment metadata from R2
  5. Reads feed items from R2
  6. Returns HTML string
        ↓
VF page injects HTML into a div — rendered inline on Case page
THE VISUALFORCE PAGE CODE

This deploys via Metadata API. No Apex controller needed. No test classes. No package.

html
<!-- VaultViewer.page -->
<apex:page 
  standardController="Case" 
  showHeader="false" 
  sidebar="false"
  lightningStylesheets="true">

<style>
  .vault-container {
    font-family: 'Salesforce Sans', Arial, sans-serif;
    font-size: 13px;
    color: #16325c;
    padding: 8px 0;
  }
  .vault-empty { display: none; }
  .vault-loading {
    color: #706e6b;
    padding: 12px;
    font-style: italic;
  }
  .vault-email-thread { }
  .vault-email-card {
    border-bottom: 1px solid #e0e5ee;
    padding: 12px 0;
    margin-bottom: 4px;
  }
  .vault-email-header {
    display: flex;
    justify-content: space-between;
    margin-bottom: 6px;
    color: #54698d;
    font-size: 12px;
  }
  .vault-email-from { font-weight: bold; color: #16325c; }
  .vault-email-body {
    color: #3c3c3c;
    line-height: 1.5;
    max-height: 120px;
    overflow: hidden;
    position: relative;
  }
  .vault-email-body.expanded { max-height: none; }
  .vault-expand-btn {
    color: #0070d2;
    cursor: pointer;
    font-size: 12px;
    background: none;
    border: none;
    padding: 4px 0;
  }
  .vault-attachments {
    margin-top: 12px;
    padding-top: 12px;
    border-top: 1px solid #e0e5ee;
  }
  .vault-attachment-item {
    display: flex;
    align-items: center;
    padding: 4px 0;
    gap: 8px;
  }
  .vault-attachment-link {
    color: #0070d2;
    text-decoration: none;
  }
  .vault-feed {
    margin-top: 12px;
    padding-top: 12px;
    border-top: 1px solid #e0e5ee;
  }
  .vault-feed-item {
    padding: 4px 0;
    font-size: 12px;
    color: #54698d;
  }
  .vault-restore-btn {
    margin-top: 12px;
    background: #0070d2;
    color: white;
    border: none;
    padding: 8px 16px;
    border-radius: 4px;
    cursor: pointer;
    font-size: 13px;
  }
  .vault-restore-btn:hover { background: #005fb2; }
  .vault-error {
    color: #c23934;
    padding: 8px;
    font-size: 12px;
  }
  .vault-section-title {
    font-weight: bold;
    color: #16325c;
    font-size: 12px;
    text-transform: uppercase;
    letter-spacing: 0.5px;
    margin: 12px 0 6px 0;
  }
</style>

<div class="vault-container" id="vault-root">
  <div class="vault-loading" id="vault-loading">
    Loading archived content...
  </div>
</div>

<script>
(function() {
  var CASE_ID = '{!Case.Id}';
  var IS_ARCHIVED = '{!Case.vault__Is_Archived__c}';
  var SESSION_ID = '{!$Api.Session_ID}';
  var ORG_ID = '{!$Organization.Id}';
  var VAULT_URL = 'https://vault.com';

  var root = document.getElementById('vault-root');
  var loading = document.getElementById('vault-loading');

  // If not archived, hide this entire section (zero height)
  if (IS_ARCHIVED !== 'true') {
    root.style.display = 'none';
    // Also collapse the VF section wrapper if possible
    try {
      var parent = root;
      while (parent && !parent.classList.contains('slds-section')) {
        parent = parent.parentElement;
      }
      if (parent) parent.style.display = 'none';
    } catch(e) {}
    return;
  }

  // Fetch archived content from Vault API
  fetch(VAULT_URL + '/api/sf-inline/' + CASE_ID, {
    headers: {
      'Authorization': 'Bearer ' + SESSION_ID,
      'X-SF-Org-Id': ORG_ID,
      'Content-Type': 'application/json'
    }
  })
  .then(function(r) {
    if (!r.ok) throw new Error('HTTP ' + r.status);
    return r.json();
  })
  .then(function(data) {
    loading.style.display = 'none';
    render(data);
  })
  .catch(function(err) {
    loading.style.display = 'none';
    root.innerHTML = '<div class="vault-error">Could not load archived content: ' 
                   + err.message + '</div>';
  });

  function render(data) {
    var html = '';

    // Email thread
    if (data.emails && data.emails.length > 0) {
      html += '<div class="vault-section-title">Archived Emails (' 
           + data.emails.length + ')</div>';
      html += '<div class="vault-email-thread">';
      
      data.emails.forEach(function(email, i) {
        var collapsed = i >= 2; // Show first 2 expanded, rest collapsed
        html += '<div class="vault-email-card">';
        html += '<div class="vault-email-header">';
        html += '<span><span class="vault-email-from">' 
             + escHtml(email.FromName || email.FromAddress) + '</span>';
        html += ' &rarr; ' + escHtml(email.ToAddress) + '</span>';
        html += '<span>' + formatDate(email.MessageDate) + '</span>';
        html += '</div>';
        html += '<div style="font-size:12px;color:#54698d;margin-bottom:4px;">' 
             + escHtml(email.Subject) + '</div>';
        
        if (email.TextBody) {
          html += '<div class="vault-email-body' 
               + (collapsed ? '' : '') + '" id="body-' + i + '">';
          // Render HTML body in sandboxed iframe to prevent XSS
          html += '<iframe srcdoc="' + escAttr(email.HtmlBody || escHtml(email.TextBody)) 
               + '" style="width:100%;border:none;min-height:80px;" '
               + 'sandbox="allow-same-origin" '
               + 'onload="this.style.height=this.contentDocument.body.scrollHeight+\'px\'"></iframe>';
          html += '</div>';
        }
        html += '</div>';
      });
      html += '</div>';
    }

    // Attachments
    if (data.attachments && data.attachments.length > 0) {
      html += '<div class="vault-section-title">Attachments (' 
           + data.attachments.length + ')</div>';
      html += '<div class="vault-attachments">';
      data.attachments.forEach(function(att) {
        html += '<div class="vault-attachment-item">';
        html += '<span>📄</span>';
        // Download link calls your API which returns a presigned URL
        html += '<a class="vault-attachment-link" '
             + 'href="' + VAULT_URL + '/api/download?caseId=' + CASE_ID 
             + '&cvId=' + encodeURIComponent(att.contentVersionId)
             + '&token=' + encodeURIComponent(SESSION_ID) + '" '
             + 'target="_blank">' + escHtml(att.filename) + '</a>';
        html += '<span style="color:#706e6b;font-size:11px;">' 
             + formatSize(att.size) + '</span>';
        html += '</div>';
      });
      html += '</div>';
    }

    // Feed comments
    if (data.feedItems && data.feedItems.length > 0) {
      html += '<div class="vault-section-title">Chatter Activity (' 
           + data.feedItems.length + ')</div>';
      html += '<div class="vault-feed">';
      data.feedItems.forEach(function(item) {
        html += '<div class="vault-feed-item">';
        html += '<strong>' + escHtml(item.CreatedByName || 'User') + '</strong>: ';
        html += escHtml(item.Body || '');
        html += '<span style="float:right">' + formatDate(item.CreatedDate) + '</span>';
        html += '</div>';
      });
      html += '</div>';
    }

    // Restore button
    html += '<button class="vault-restore-btn" onclick="vaultRestore()">Restore to Salesforce</button>';
    html += '<span id="vault-restore-status" style="margin-left:8px;font-size:12px;"></span>';

    root.innerHTML = html;
  }

  window.vaultRestore = function() {
    var btn = document.querySelector('.vault-restore-btn');
    var status = document.getElementById('vault-restore-status');
    btn.disabled = true;
    btn.textContent = 'Restoring...';
    
    fetch(VAULT_URL + '/api/restore', {
      method: 'POST',
      headers: {
        'Authorization': 'Bearer ' + SESSION_ID,
        'X-SF-Org-Id': ORG_ID,
        'Content-Type': 'application/json'
      },
      body: JSON.stringify({ caseIds: [CASE_ID] })
    })
    .then(function(r) { return r.json(); })
    .then(function(data) {
      status.textContent = 'Restore started. Refresh the page in a moment.';
      status.style.color = '#04844b';
    })
    .catch(function(err) {
      btn.disabled = false;
      btn.textContent = 'Restore to Salesforce';
      status.textContent = 'Error: ' + err.message;
      status.style.color = '#c23934';
    });
  };

  function escHtml(str) {
    if (!str) return '';
    return String(str)
      .replace(/&/g, '&amp;')
      .replace(/</g, '&lt;')
      .replace(/>/g, '&gt;')
      .replace(/"/g, '&quot;');
  }

  function escAttr(str) {
    if (!str) return '';
    return String(str).replace(/"/g, '&quot;').replace(/'/g, '&#39;');
  }

  function formatDate(dateStr) {
    if (!dateStr) return '';
    var d = new Date(dateStr);
    return d.toLocaleDateString('en-US', { 
      month: 'short', day: 'numeric', year: 'numeric',
      hour: '2-digit', minute: '2-digit'
    });
  }

  function formatSize(bytes) {
    if (!bytes) return '';
    if (bytes < 1024) return bytes + ' B';
    if (bytes < 1048576) return Math.round(bytes / 1024) + ' KB';
    return (bytes / 1048576).toFixed(1) + ' MB';
  }

})();
</script>
</apex:page>

  <types>

    <name>ApexPage</name>
  </types>

  <availableInTouch>true</availableInTouch>
  <confirmationTokenRequired>false</confirmationTokenRequired>
  <label>Vault Archived Content</label>
</ApexPage>
THE NEW HONO API ENDPOINT

You need one new endpoint that returns JSON (not HTML) so the VF page can render it natively using SF-styled components:

GET /api/sf-inline/:caseId
Headers: Authorization: Bearer {SF session token}
         X-SF-Org-Id: {org ID}

Response:
{
  "caseId": "500xx...",
  "archivedAt": "2024-01-15T14:30:00Z",
  "emails": [
    {
      "Id": "02sxx...",
      "Subject": "Re: Invoice Issue",
      "FromAddress": "customer@acme.com",
      "FromName": "John Smith",
      "ToAddress": "support@client.com",
      "MessageDate": "2023-01-15T14:30:00Z",
      "HtmlBody": "<html>...</html>",
      "TextBody": "Hi, I still haven't..."
    }
  ],
  "attachments": [
    {
      "contentVersionId": "068xx...",
      "filename": "invoice_44821.pdf",
      "mimeType": "application/pdf",
      "size": 430080
    }
  ],
  "feedItems": [
    {
      "Id": "0D5xx...",
      "Body": "Escalated to billing team",
      "CreatedDate": "2023-01-16T10:00:00Z",
      "CreatedByName": "Sarah K."
    }
  ]
}
For attachment downloads, /api/download validates the session, then returns a redirect to a 30-second presigned R2 URL. The agent clicks Download, gets the file. No vault.com page opened, no new tab for viewing — just a standard file download.

THE ONE MANUAL STEP FROM ADMIN

After your Metadata API deploy, the admin does this once:

Setup → Object Manager → Case → Page Layouts → [their layout]
  
  Scroll to bottom of layout editor
  In left panel: "Visualforce Pages" section
  Drag "Vault Archived Content" onto the layout
  Set height: 400px (adjustable)
  Check "Show scrollbars"
  Save
That is it. One drag. 30 seconds. After this every Case opened by any agent in that org shows the archived content inline automatically.

WHAT THIS GIVES YOU VS WHAT LWC GIVES

Visualforce approach (Phase 1):
  ✓ Inline on Case page — agent never leaves SF
  ✓ Shows emails, attachments, feed items, restore button
  ✓ Deployed via Metadata API — no package, no install URL
  ✓ No Apex, no test classes, no coverage requirement
  ✓ Works on all SF editions (Enterprise, Unlimited, Professional+)
  ✗ One manual step: admin drags VF section onto page layout
  ✗ Slightly dated VF rendering vs native Lightning feel
  ✗ Cannot fully match Lightning design system perfectly
  ✗ If Case page uses only "Highlights Panel + Custom" layout with
    no classic Record Detail, VF section may not be available

LWC approach (Phase 2):
  ✓ Inline on Case page
  ✓ Fully native Lightning look and feel
  ✓ Can be auto-placed via package installation
  ✗ Requires managed package + install URL
  ✗ More build effort (LWC + Apex controller + test class)
  ✗ Managed package release cycle slower
The VF approach solves the core problem: agent sees everything inline without leaving Salesforce. The LWC in Phase 2 makes it look better and eliminates the one manual admin step.

Good. You want this to work on modern Lightning Experience pages, not just Classic layouts. That changes everything about the approach.

THE HARD TRUTH FIRST


Searched the web
Searched the web
Now I have the complete picture. Let me give you the definitive answer.

THE REAL ANSWER

You must enable "Available for Lightning Experience, Lightning Communities, and the mobile app" for a Visualforce page to make it available in the Lightning App Builder. When you create a custom app page in the Lightning App Builder, you can add a Visualforce page to the page by using the Visualforce component. 
Cloudflare

You can extend page layouts by embedding Visualforce pages on them to display completely custom content on a standard page. The behavior is identical to Salesforce Classic, except you need to view the record's Details tab to see the page layout. 
Blogger

So the path to modern Lightning Experience is clear. Two separate things:

Path A — Page Layout section (same as before, works in Lightning via the Details tab): VF page embedded in the classic page layout, visible on the Details tab of a Lightning record page. Works everywhere, no Lightning App Builder needed.

Path B — Lightning App Builder component (fully modern, any region of the Lightning page): VF page added directly as a Lightning component in Lightning App Builder, placeable anywhere — sidebar, main column, tab, bottom section. Requires the "Available for Lightning Experience" checkbox enabled on the VF page.

Since Visualforce is a standard component to choose from within Lightning App Builder, getting a VF page ready for selection is simply having the "Available for Lightning Experience" checkbox checked, plus adding the lightningStylesheets attribute to the <apex:page> tag to style the VF page with the look of Lightning Experience. 
Cloudflare Community

This is the path. And you can set that checkbox programmatically in your Metadata API deployment.

HOW YOU SET IT VIA METADATA API

In your VaultViewer.page-meta.xml:

xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<ApexPage xmlns="http://soap.sforce.com/2006/04/metadata">
    <apiVersion>66.0</apiVersion>
    <availableInTouch>true</availableInTouch>
    <confirmationTokenRequired>false</confirmationTokenRequired>
    <label>Vault Archived Content</label>
</ApexPage>
availableInTouch is the metadata equivalent of that Lightning Experience checkbox. Setting it true makes the VF page available as a Lightning App Builder component and on mobile. Deployable in your existing Metadata API zip — no extra steps.

HOW THE AGENT ACTUALLY EXPERIENCES IT

After your Metadata API deployment, the admin opens Lightning App Builder for the Case record page and drags a "Visualforce" standard component to any region. They pick "Vault Archived Content" from the dropdown. Done.

What the agent sees on a modern Lightning record page:

THE COMPLETE VF PAGE — LIGHTNING STYLED

html
<apex:page 
  standardController="Case"
  showHeader="false"
  sidebar="false"
  lightningStylesheets="true"
  applyBodyTag="false"
  applyHtmlTag="false">

<apex:slds />

<div class="slds-scope">
  <div id="vault-root">
    <div id="vault-loading" class="slds-text-color_weak slds-p-around_small">
      <div class="slds-spinner_container" style="position:relative;height:40px;">
        <div role="status" class="slds-spinner slds-spinner_small">
          <span class="slds-assistive-text">Loading</span>
          <div class="slds-spinner__dot-a"></div>
          <div class="slds-spinner__dot-b"></div>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>
</div>

<script>
(function() {
  var CASE_ID    = '{!JSENCODE(Case.Id)}';
  var IS_ARCHIVED = '{!Case.vault__Is_Archived__c}';
  var SESSION_ID  = '{!JSENCODE($Api.Session_ID)}';
  var ORG_ID      = '{!JSENCODE($Organization.Id)}';
  var VAULT_URL   = 'https://vault.com';

  var root    = document.getElementById('vault-root');
  var loading = document.getElementById('vault-loading');

  // Non-archived case — render nothing, hide component
  if (IS_ARCHIVED !== 'true') {
    root.style.display = 'none';
    return;
  }

  fetch(VAULT_URL + '/api/sf-inline/' + CASE_ID, {
    headers: {
      'Authorization': 'Bearer ' + SESSION_ID,
      'X-SF-Org-Id': ORG_ID
    }
  })
  .then(function(r) {
    if (!r.ok) throw new Error('Status ' + r.status);
    return r.json();
  })
  .then(function(data) {
    loading.style.display = 'none';
    renderVault(data);
  })
  .catch(function(err) {
    loading.style.display = 'none';
    root.innerHTML = 
      '<div class="slds-notify slds-notify_alert slds-alert_error slds-m-around_small" role="alert">' +
      '<span class="slds-assistive-text">Error</span>' +
      '<h2>Could not load archived content: ' + escHtml(err.message) + '</h2>' +
      '</div>';
  });

  function renderVault(data) {
    var html = '<div class="slds-scope slds-p-around_small">';

    // ── EMAIL THREAD ─────────────────────────────────────────
    if (data.emails && data.emails.length > 0) {
      html += '<div class="slds-section slds-is-open">';
      html += '<h3 class="slds-section__title">';
      html += '<span class="slds-truncate slds-p-horizontal_small">';
      html += 'Archived Emails (' + data.emails.length + ')';
      html += '</span></h3>';
      html += '<div class="slds-section__content">';

      data.emails.forEach(function(email, idx) {
        var isIncoming = email.Incoming === 'true' || email.Incoming === true;

        html += '<div class="slds-box slds-box_x-small slds-m-bottom_x-small" ';
        html += 'style="border-left: 3px solid ' + (isIncoming ? '#0070d2' : '#04844b') + '">';

        // Email header row
        html += '<div class="slds-grid slds-gutters_x-small slds-m-bottom_x-small">';
        html += '<div class="slds-col">';
        html += '<p class="slds-text-body_small">';
        html += '<strong>' + escHtml(email.FromName || email.FromAddress) + '</strong>';
        html += '<span class="slds-text-color_weak"> &rarr; ' + escHtml(email.ToAddress) + '</span>';
        html += '</p>';
        html += '<p class="slds-text-title slds-text-color_weak">' + escHtml(email.Subject) + '</p>';
        html += '</div>';
        html += '<div class="slds-col slds-shrink-none slds-text-align_right">';
        html += '<p class="slds-text-title slds-text-color_weak">' + formatDate(email.MessageDate) + '</p>';
        html += '</div>';
        html += '</div>';

        // HTML body — rendered in sandboxed iframe
        // This is the KEY part: actual rendered HTML body, not plain text
        if (email.HtmlBody) {
          var safeHtml = email.HtmlBody
            .replace(/\\/g, '\\\\')
            .replace(/'/g, "\\'")
            .replace(/\r?\n/g, '\\n');
          
          html += '<div class="slds-m-top_x-small" style="border:1px solid #e0e5ee;border-radius:4px;overflow:hidden;">';
          html += '<iframe ';
          html += 'id="email-frame-' + idx + '" ';
          html += 'style="width:100%;min-height:100px;border:none;display:block;" ';
          html += 'sandbox="allow-same-origin allow-popups" ';
          html += 'srcdoc="" ';
          html += 'onload="this.style.height=Math.min(this.contentDocument.body.scrollHeight+16,400)+\'px\'">';
          html += '</iframe>';
          html += '</div>';
          
          // Set srcdoc via JS to avoid HTML escaping issues
          html += '<script>(function(){';
          html += 'var f=document.getElementById("email-frame-' + idx + '");';
          html += 'if(f)f.srcdoc=\'' + safeHtml + '\';';
          html += '})()</s' + 'cript>';
        } else if (email.TextBody) {
          html += '<div class="slds-text-body_small slds-m-top_x-small" ';
          html += 'style="white-space:pre-wrap;max-height:200px;overflow-y:auto;';
          html += 'padding:8px;background:#f4f6f9;border-radius:4px;">';
          html += escHtml(email.TextBody);
          html += '</div>';
        }

        html += '</div>'; // slds-box
      });

      html += '</div></div>'; // section content + section
    }

    // ── ATTACHMENTS ──────────────────────────────────────────
    if (data.attachments && data.attachments.length > 0) {
      html += '<div class="slds-section slds-is-open slds-m-top_small">';
      html += '<h3 class="slds-section__title">';
      html += '<span class="slds-truncate slds-p-horizontal_small">';
      html += 'Attachments (' + data.attachments.length + ')';
      html += '</span></h3>';
      html += '<div class="slds-section__content">';
      html += '<ul class="slds-list_dotted">';

      data.attachments.forEach(function(att) {
        var icon = getFileIcon(att.mimeType);
        var dlUrl = VAULT_URL + '/api/download'
          + '?caseId=' + encodeURIComponent(CASE_ID)
          + '&cvId=' + encodeURIComponent(att.contentVersionId)
          + '&token=' + encodeURIComponent(SESSION_ID);

        html += '<li class="slds-item slds-p-vertical_xx-small">';
        html += '<div class="slds-grid slds-gutters_x-small slds-grid_vertical-align-center">';
        html += '<div class="slds-col slds-shrink-none" style="width:24px">' + icon + '</div>';
        html += '<div class="slds-col">';
        html += '<a href="' + dlUrl + '" target="_blank" ';
        html += 'class="slds-truncate" style="max-width:200px;display:block;">';
        html += escHtml(att.filename);
        html += '</a>';
        html += '<span class="slds-text-title slds-text-color_weak">' + formatSize(att.size) + '</span>';
        html += '</div>';
        html += '</div>';
        html += '</li>';
      });

      html += '</ul>';
      html += '</div></div>';
    }

    // ── CHATTER / FEED ───────────────────────────────────────
    if (data.feedItems && data.feedItems.length > 0) {
      html += '<div class="slds-section slds-is-open slds-m-top_small">';
      html += '<h3 class="slds-section__title">';
      html += '<span class="slds-truncate slds-p-horizontal_small">';
      html += 'Chatter (' + data.feedItems.length + ')';
      html += '</span></h3>';
      html += '<div class="slds-section__content">';

      data.feedItems.forEach(function(item) {
        html += '<div class="slds-media slds-m-bottom_x-small">';
        html += '<div class="slds-media__figure">';
        html += '<span class="slds-avatar slds-avatar_small" style="background:#0070d2;color:white;';
        html += 'display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;font-size:11px;">';
        html += escHtml((item.CreatedByName || 'U')[0].toUpperCase());
        html += '</span>';
        html += '</div>';
        html += '<div class="slds-media__body">';
        html += '<p class="slds-text-body_small">';
        html += '<strong>' + escHtml(item.CreatedByName || 'User') + '</strong> ';
        html += '<span class="slds-text-color_weak">' + formatDate(item.CreatedDate) + '</span>';
        html += '</p>';
        html += '<p class="slds-text-body_small">' + escHtml(item.Body || '') + '</p>';
        html += '</div>';
        html += '</div>';
      });

      html += '</div></div>';
    }

    // ── RESTORE BUTTON ───────────────────────────────────────
    html += '<div class="slds-m-top_small">';
    html += '<button class="slds-button slds-button_brand" onclick="vaultRestore()">';
    html += 'Restore to Salesforce';
    html += '</button>';
    html += '<span id="vault-restore-msg" class="slds-m-left_small slds-text-body_small"></span>';
    html += '</div>';

    html += '</div>'; // slds-scope wrapper

    root.innerHTML = html;
  }

  window.vaultRestore = function() {
    var btn = document.querySelector('.slds-button_brand');
    var msg = document.getElementById('vault-restore-msg');
    if (!btn) return;
    btn.disabled = true;
    btn.textContent = 'Restoring...';

    fetch(VAULT_URL + '/api/restore', {
      method: 'POST',
      headers: {
        'Authorization': 'Bearer ' + SESSION_ID,
        'X-SF-Org-Id': ORG_ID,
        'Content-Type': 'application/json'
      },
      body: JSON.stringify({ caseIds: [CASE_ID] })
    })
    .then(function(r) { return r.json(); })
    .then(function() {
      msg.textContent = 'Restore queued. Refresh the page in ~2 minutes.';
      msg.style.color = '#04844b';
    })
    .catch(function(err) {
      btn.disabled = false;
      btn.textContent = 'Restore to Salesforce';
      msg.textContent = 'Error: ' + err.message;
      msg.style.color = '#c23934';
    });
  };

  // ── HELPERS ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  function escHtml(s) {
    if (!s) return '';
    return String(s)
      .replace(/&/g,'&amp;')
      .replace(/</g,'&lt;')
      .replace(/>/g,'&gt;')
      .replace(/"/g,'&quot;')
      .replace(/'/g,'&#39;');
  }

  function formatDate(s) {
    if (!s) return '';
    try {
      var d = new Date(s);
      return d.toLocaleString('en-US',{
        month:'short',day:'numeric',year:'numeric',
        hour:'2-digit',minute:'2-digit'
      });
    } catch(e) { return s; }
  }

  function formatSize(b) {
    if (!b) return '';
    if (b < 1024) return b + ' B';
    if (b < 1048576) return Math.round(b/1024) + ' KB';
    return (b/1048576).toFixed(1) + ' MB';
  }

  function getFileIcon(mime) {
    if (!mime) return '📄';
    if (mime.includes('pdf')) return '📕';
    if (mime.includes('image')) return '🖼';
    if (mime.includes('word') || mime.includes('document')) return '📝';
    if (mime.includes('sheet') || mime.includes('excel')) return '📊';
    if (mime.includes('zip') || mime.includes('compressed')) return '🗜';
    return '📄';
  }

})();
</script>
</apex:page>
WHAT THIS LOOKS LIKE IN THE LIGHTNING APP BUILDER

Admin opens Lightning App Builder for Case record page

Drag "Visualforce" component to any region on the page

The admin does this once. After that every agent on that org sees archived content inline, styled with Lightning Design System, looking native.

YOUR API ENDPOINT FOR THIS

GET /api/sf-inline/:caseId returns JSON. The VF page renders it client-side using SLDS classes. No HTML rendering on your server — the VF page does the rendering in the browser using SF's own design system.

The email HTML bodies are rendered in sandboxed iframes inside the VF page. The iframe uses srcdoc to inject the HTML — no external URL needed, no X-Frame-Options issues, no CSP violations. The email renders exactly as it appeared in Salesforce originally.

SUMMARY: WHAT YOU GET WITHOUT LWC

✓ Works on modern Lightning Experience pages — not just Classic
✓ Deployable via Metadata API — no managed package, no install URL
✓ Styled with Lightning Design System — looks native, not dated
✓ Rendered HTML email bodies inline — exactly as they appeared before archiving
✓ Attachments listed with direct download links
✓ Chatter/feed items shown chronologically
✓ Restore button inline
✓ For non-archived cases — component is invisible (zero height)
✓ One admin step: drag component to page in Lightning App Builder (2 minutes)
✓ availableInTouch: true in metadata — also works on Salesforce mobile app
The only thing LWC gives you that this doesn't: auto-placement without the admin drag-and-drop step, and marginally better event handling. For Phase 1 the VF approach gives agents everything they need, on modern Lightning, looking correct. LWC in Phase 2 removes that one admin step.

THE CLAUDE CODE PROMPTS

PROMPT 10B — VaultViewer Visualforce Page + sf-inline endpoint

Run this after Prompt 10 (SF OAuth + Setup Routes) and before Prompt 11 (Jobs Routes):

Build the VaultViewer Visualforce page and its API endpoint.

--- packages/sf-client/src/metadata.ts (UPDATE existing deployOrgSetup) ---

Add to the Metadata API zip:
  pages/VaultViewer.page
  pages/VaultViewer.page-meta.xml

VaultViewer.page-meta.xml:
  apiVersion: 66.0
  availableInTouch: true       ← makes it available in Lightning App Builder
  confirmationTokenRequired: false
  label: Vault Archived Content

VaultViewer.page content (pure Visualforce, NO Apex controller):
  standardController="Case"
  showHeader="false"
  sidebar="false"
  lightningStylesheets="true"
  applyBodyTag="false"
  applyHtmlTag="false"

  Uses these SF merge fields (no Apex needed):
    {!JSENCODE(Case.Id)}
    {!Case.vault__Is_Archived__c}
    {!JSENCODE($Api.Session_ID)}
    {!JSENCODE($Organization.Id)}

  JavaScript behavior:
    If vault__Is_Archived__c != 'true': set root display:none, return
    Else: fetch('https://vault.com/api/sf-inline/' + caseId, {
      headers: {
        Authorization: 'Bearer ' + sessionId,
        'X-SF-Org-Id': orgId
      }
    })
    
  Renders response JSON using SLDS utility classes:
    Emails section: each email as slds-box with:
      - Header row: sender name, recipient, date (slds-grid layout)
      - Subject line in slds-text-title
      - HtmlBody rendered in: <iframe sandbox="allow-same-origin allow-popups"
        style="width:100%;border:none;" onload="auto-height">
        srcdoc set via JavaScript (not HTML attribute) to avoid escaping issues
      - TextBody fallback if HtmlBody absent (pre-wrap div)
    
    Attachments section: slds-list with filename, size, download link
      Download link: https://vault.com/api/download?caseId=X&cvId=Y&token=Z
    
    Feed section: slds-media layout with avatar initial, author, date, body
    
    Restore button: slds-button slds-button_brand
      onclick calls POST https://vault.com/api/restore with fetch
      Shows inline status: 'Restore queued. Refresh in ~2 minutes.'

  SECURITY RULES for this VF page:
    - Never use innerHTML with raw email content
    - Always set iframe srcdoc via JavaScript assignment, not HTML attribute
    - iframe sandbox MUST include allow-same-origin, MUST NOT include allow-scripts
    - Escape all values from API response before any DOM insertion (escHtml helper)
    - JSENCODE() all SF merge field values to prevent VF injection

--- apps/worker/src/routes/sf-inline.ts (NEW FILE) ---

GET /api/sf-inline/:caseId
  Query param: (none — caseId from path, session from header)
  Headers: Authorization: Bearer {SF session token}
           X-SF-Org-Id: {org ID}

  1. Validate SF session:
     GET {sfInstanceUrl}/services/oauth2/userinfo
     Headers: Authorization: Bearer {token}
     If fails: return 401
     Extract: orgId (18-char) from response

  2. Match org:
     SELECT * FROM sfOrgs WHERE sfOrgId = orgId
     If not found: return 403
     Get tenantId from the org record

  3. Check caseId is archived:
     SELECT * FROM jobRecords WHERE sfRecordId = caseId
     AND status = 'archived'
     JOIN jobs ON jobs.id = jobRecords.jobId WHERE jobs.orgId = org.id
     If not found: return 404 with { archived: false }

  4. Read from R2 (parallel):
     List: /{tenantId}/Case/{caseId}/emails/ → GET all *.json files
     List: /{tenantId}/Case/{caseId}/attachments/*.meta.json → GET all
     GET: /{tenantId}/Case/{caseId}/feed/items.json

  5. Sort emails by MessageDate ascending

  6. Return JSON:
     {
       archived: true,
       archivedAt: jobRecord.updatedAt,
       emails: [{ Id, Subject, FromAddress, FromName, ToAddress, 
                  MessageDate, HtmlBody, TextBody, Incoming }],
       attachments: [{ contentVersionId, filename, mimeType, size }],
       feedItems: [{ Id, Body, CreatedDate, CreatedByName }]
     }

--- apps/worker/src/routes/download.ts (NEW FILE) ---

GET /api/download
  Query: caseId, cvId, token (SF session token)
  
  1. Validate SF session token (same as sf-inline)
  2. Verify caseId is archived and belongs to this org (same as sf-inline)
  3. Verify cvId exists in R2 meta for this case:
     GET /{tenantId}/Case/{caseId}/attachments/{cvId}.meta.json
  4. Generate R2 presigned URL with 30 second expiry
  5. Return HTTP 302 redirect to presigned URL
  
  Never serve binary directly from Worker — always redirect to R2.

Mount both routes in apps/worker/src/index.ts.
Run tsc --noEmit after writing.
PROMPT 10C — Security Middleware

Run this after 10B, before Prompt 11:

Build the security middleware layer. This must run on ALL routes.

apps/worker/src/middleware/security.ts

1. securityHeadersMiddleware: MiddlewareHandler
   Adds to every response:
   c.header('Strict-Transport-Security', 'max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains')
   c.header('X-Content-Type-Options', 'nosniff')
   c.header('X-Frame-Options', 'DENY')
   c.header('Referrer-Policy', 'strict-origin-when-cross-origin')
   c.header('Permissions-Policy', 'camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=()')
   
   For routes starting with /api/sf-inline/ or /api/download:
     Skip X-Frame-Options (called from Salesforce VF page context)
     CSP: "default-src 'self'; frame-src 'self'"
   
   For /sf-view/ routes:
     X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN (may be opened inside SF tab)
   
   For all other routes:
     CSP: "default-src 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'"

2. rateLimitMiddleware: factory function
   rateLimitMiddleware({ limit, windowMs, keyFn })
   
   Uses D1 to store counters:
   Table: rateLimits { key TEXT PK, count INTEGER, windowStart INTEGER }
   
   On each request:
     key = keyFn(c)  (e.g. IP address, or userId, or tenantId)
     current = SELECT count, windowStart FROM rateLimits WHERE key = key
     If windowStart < now - windowMs: reset (new window)
     If count >= limit: return 429 { error: 'Too many requests', retryAfter: X }
     Else: upsert count + 1, continue
   
   Apply with different configs per route group (mount in index.ts):
     /api/auth/*: limit=10, windowMs=60000, keyFn = IP
     /api/jobs POST: limit=5, windowMs=60000, keyFn = tenantId
     /api/sf-inline/*: limit=120, windowMs=60000, keyFn = X-SF-Org-Id header
     /api/download: limit=60, windowMs=60000, keyFn = session userId
     All /api/*: limit=300, windowMs=60000, keyFn = userId or IP

3. Add rateLimits table to packages/db/src/schema.ts:
   rateLimits:
     key TEXT PK
     count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1
     windowStart INTEGER NOT NULL
   (no FK — standalone counter table)

4. validateSFRecordId(id: string): boolean
   export this as a utility used in routes to validate SF IDs before SOQL use
   Valid SF ID: 15 or 18 chars, alphanumeric only, starts with 3-char prefix
   Regex: /^[a-zA-Z0-9]{15}([a-zA-Z0-9]{3})?$/
   Throw ValidationError if fails.

Mount securityHeadersMiddleware as the FIRST middleware in apps/worker/src/index.ts.
Mount rateLimitMiddleware per route group after auth but before business logic.
Run tsc --noEmit after writing.
PROMPT 10D — Flow Detection Endpoint

Run this after 10C, before Prompt 11:

Build the Salesforce flow detection feature.

apps/worker/src/routes/sf.ts (ADD to existing file):

GET /api/sf/detect-flows
  Query: orgId
  Protected: authMiddleware + tenantMiddleware
  
  Purpose: detect active Salesforce Flows that could fire on EmailMessage
  deletion and need the vault__Restore_In_Progress__c bypass condition.
  
  1. Get admin token from D1 sfTokens (tokenType='admin_setup')
  2. Query SF:
     SELECT Id, Label, Description, ProcessType
     FROM FlowDefinition
     WHERE TriggerObjectOrEventLabel = 'EmailMessage'
     AND Status = 'Active'
  3. Also query for flows on Case that could react to EmailMessage deletion:
     SELECT Id, Label, ProcessType
     FROM FlowDefinition  
     WHERE TriggerObjectOrEventLabel = 'Case'
     AND Status = 'Active'
     AND TriggerType IN ('RecordBeforeUpdate', 'RecordAfterUpdate')
  4. Return:
     {
       emailMessageFlows: [{ id, label, processType }],
       caseFlows: [{ id, label, processType }],
       hasRiskyFlows: emailMessageFlows.length > 0,
       recommendation: "Add entry condition vault__Restore_In_Progress__c = false 
                        to the following flows before running archive jobs: ..."
     }
  5. Cache result in D1 sfCache key='{orgId}:flow_detection' TTL=24h

This endpoint is called:
  - During onboarding after org setup completes
  - From the Archive page before the first archive job
  - On-demand from Settings page

In apps/worker/src/workflows/archive.ts (MODIFY Phase 3 step):
  Before the EmailMessage bulk delete step:
    1. Get all parent Case IDs being deleted
    2. Bulk API Update: vault__Restore_In_Progress__c = true on these Cases
    3. Execute EmailMessage bulk delete (existing logic)
    4. Bulk API Update: vault__Restore_In_Progress__c = false on these Cases
  
  This runs regardless of whether risky flows were detected.
  It is always the safe approach and adds minimal API cost.

Run tsc --noEmit after writing.
